                                     Appendix C

             Table A–Austere Qualification

    The austere qualification event, called Table A, is a record course of fire
    used to determine the live-fire proficiency of a crew platform. Table A
    serves as the live-fire proficiency event for readiness reporting for units
    requiring crew qualification in extreme circumstances while deployed. It is
    used by exception only, with permission from the first general officer in the
    chain of command.

    It serves as the live-fire prerequisite for the crew to move to collective
    live-fire events. Table A, Austere Qualification, is a live environment-
    training event under live-fire conditions using full-caliber training
    ammunition, austere targetry at half distance (1/2-scale range to target) and
    engagement scenarios, at a combat tempo, conducted on an authorized
    live-fire facility.

    This table reinforces the basic skills trained during previous events but is
    tailored for use on non-standard, host nation live-fire training facilities that
    do not meet the scenario requirements in chapter 6

OVERVIEW OF TABLE A

     C-1. Table A, Austere Qualification, is conducted from the crew’s vehicle and uses
     full-caliber training ammunition on an authorized live-fire facility or safety certified
     training area. Table A is used to train and evaluate the crew’s ability to engage stationary
     or moving targets placed in a tactical array, during day and limited visibility conditions,
     with an operational or simulated degraded fire control system, as applicable. Crews
     execute these tasks from the offense and defense using full-caliber ammunition with
     scaled targetry at half-scale distances.

     C-2. This table requires each crew to successfully complete the live-fire prerequisites
     and all screening and zeroing procedures for their platform. The crew may complete this
     course without any additional live-fire training at an increased risk.

                                     Appendix D

         Table B–Basic Skills Engagements

    Basic skills engagements, called Table B, are designed to assist the
    commander by providing additional on-demand training for those
    retically-challenged crews prior to advancing to Table V. These
    engagements are built as a training stepping stone to Table V for crews
    experiencing difficulty on specific live-fire capabilities tasks. The basic
    skills engagements are a menu-based, commander-selected engagements to
    incorporate into the unit training plan. They augment the engagements on
    Table IV when retraining on failed tasks will not best prepare the crew for
    advancement to Table V, Practice, or utilization of a simulator system are
    unavailable.
    These engagements are focused on the five capabilities-based tasks for the
    BFV, VC, machine gun, main gun, change of weapon system or
    ammunition, and degraded skills. Commanders select any engagement for
    integration into their training plan based on available resources (time and
    training ammunition) to provide experience to struggling crews prior to
    Table V, Practice.

OVERVIEW OF TABLE B

     D-1. Table B provides crews the ability to train on specific capability-based skills they
     are experiencing difficulty. Rather than refire failed engagements on Table IV, Basic,
     the commander can select an appropriate Table B engagement that builds additional
     rigor and skills during retraining opportunities.

     D-2. These engagements are not resourced. They are provided as a training tool for
     commanders to conduct retraining and cross-training opportunities with additional
     variety and increased rigor to best prepare crews for Table V.

     D-3. The planning and preparation process, including the uniform, facilities, targetry,
     and range support requirements are the same as Table IV, Basic. See chapter 4 for
     detailed information for those processes.

BASIC SKILLS AMMUNITION REQUIREMENTS

     D-4. Each engagement has a separate ammunition requirement. Those requirements
     are listed for each engagement separately. (See figure D-1 on page 346 for an overview
     of all 10 engagement’s ammunition requirements.)

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  345

     10 engagements.)

                                      Appendix E

           Table C–Complex Engagements

    Table C, Complex Engagements, is a course of fire developed for
    commanders to challenge their best crews. Table C serves as a live-fire
    check on mastery skills on a BFV platform. It is intended for use after a
    crew has successfully qualified on their vehicle within the past 6 months.
    The engagements were specifically designed by Master Gunners to focus on
    the capabilities of the BFV, its ammunition, and fire control system
    functionality. It should only be used for crews that have demonstrated an
    exceptional level of skill with their vehicle.
    This event is scored in the same manner as other engagements in this
    publication. Units electing to execute this course of fire will require detailed
    coordination with their supporting range operations branch to build a safe
    and effective scenario.

OVERVIEW OF TABLE C

     E-1. Table C, Complex Engagements, is fired from the crew’s vehicle and uses
     full-caliber training ammunition on an authorized live-fire facility or safety certified
     training area. The training ammunition required to conduct Table C are not resourced.
     The unit may use training munitions available in their account or coordinate with their
     ammunition management office for additional resources, as available.

     E-2. Table C is used to train and evaluate the experienced crew’s ability to engage
     stationary or moving targets placed in a tactical array, during day and limited visibility
     conditions, with an operational or simulated degraded fire control system, as applicable.
     Crews execute these tasks from the offense and defense using full-caliber ammunition.

     E-3. This table requires each crew to successfully complete the live-fire prerequisites,
     all zeroing procedures for their platform, successfully complete Table VI, Qualification,
     and are selected by the commander to conduct this event.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  359


---

Appendix E

                            Experienced Crews Only

          Crews must be selected by the brigade commander or above to
          conduct this event. This table is designed to train and evaluate
          complex engagement skills, day and night, while fighting from the
          defense and during offensive engagements.

PURPOSE OF TABLE C

     E-4. Table C provides a series of live-fire engagements to showcase a unit’s
     experienced crews on a BFV, demonstrate their master of their profession, and to
     demonstrate their lethality in a rigorous, competitive course of fire.

METHOD–TABLE C

     E-5. Table C is a performance-based evaluation of advanced gunnery skills in a live
     environment under live-fire conditions. Conduct of Table C is from the crew’s Bradley
     using full-caliber ammunition and BESs. It utilizes the unit’s most experienced
     evaluators to educate, train, test, and retrain Soldiers to meet or exceed those standards.

END STATE–TABLE C

     E-6. The crew demonstrates mastery of their BFV across a series of demanding
     engagements. The crews are challenged by introducing them to mastery-level
     engagements designed by Master Gunners based on large-scale combat operations threat
     dilemmas. The unit receives an exceptionally well-trained crew that trains others on their
     knowledge and experiences gained.

TASK, CONDITIONS, AND STANDARDS–TABLE C

     E-7. Units may not add to the live-fire requirements or tasks associated with Table C.
     The division commander or equivalent maintains the qualification course of fire
     standards and approved scenarios. (See figure E-1 for the task, conditions, and standard
     for Table C, Qualification.)

360  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                             Table C–Complex Engagements

Figure E-1. Table C, Complex Engagements, task, conditions, and standards

PLAN FOR TABLE C

     E-8. Table C, Complex Engagements, is a live-fire training environment event using
     full-caliber and small arms training ammunition, BESs, and specific TADSS. This
     course of fire requires experienced VCEs and coordination with the higher headquarters
     for support.

                             Use Experienced VCEs

          Units should consider having experienced Master Gunners serve
          as VCEs for these engagements due to their inherent complexity.

     E-9. This section details the resources needed for planning purposes. It includes
     general information on the following topics for consideration during the planning
     process, which are—

          • Uniform—the minimum requirements for the combat vehicle crewmember’s
                fire-retardant clothing and protective equipment.

          • Facilities—available facilities that support the training event.
          • Training plan—the minimum tasks, general guidelines, and special instructions

                for the training event.
          • Time and throughput—the estimated time required to complete the training

                event including throughput considerations for planning.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  361

Appendix E

          • Ammunition—any training munitions required to complete the training event.
          • TADSS—any available training aids that directly support or augment the

                training event.

     E-10. Crews must be in the appropriate vehicle crewmember uniform or prescribed unit
     uniform and protective mask. Crews are not required to have any additional body armor,
     ballistic plates, or gear during the training event unless directed by the commander.
     (See figure E-2 for the minimum uniform training requirements this event.)

Due to the complexity and difficulty of the Table C, complex
engagements, units should only use very experienced and

and/or Master Gunners if available.

TRAINING PLAN FOR TABLE C

     E-14. The design of Table C is purposefully challenging to the crews while providing
     realistic engagements that exercise the crew’s ability to utilize the key capabilities of the
     vehicle’s fire control system. Units retrain as necessary with full-caliber ammunition
     using first-round hit savings (harvested ammunition) from the previous tables in the
     training strategy.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25                                   365


---

Appendix E

     E-15. All crew platform tables are multiple qualification criteria tables. Crews must
     score at least 70 points per target according to the target kill standards in TC 3-20.31 on
     single and multiple target engagements to qualify the engagement. Crews must also
     score more than 700 points overall with a minimum of 70 points per target during
     7 of 10 engagements on the qualification table to be used. Crews must qualify at least
     one engagement during day and one engagement during limited visibility to be rated as
     Top Gun.

     E-16. Table C uses full-caliber training ammunition on an authorized live-fire facility
     or safety certified training area that assesses an element’s tactical employment
     proficiency and lethality. Units are required to use full-scale targets at full range for the
     engagement scenarios unless specifically directed.

Scenario Requirements for Table C

     E-17. All engagements are evaluated using certified VCEs using DA Form 8265 and
     DA Form 8265-1 as the official record of training. Master Gunners must ensure the
     training records are included in each crew’s gunnery packet (see TC 3-20.31).

     E-18. The engagements listings are by capability type using the standard engagement
     naming structure listed in TC 3-20.31. It provides the requirements for each engagement,
     which are described in paragraphs E-19 through E-28.

Task Description–Table C

     E-19. The task description includes both the engagement number (the table number plus
     engagement task) followed by the system capability trained.

Firing Vehicle Posture–Table C

     E-20. The firing vehicle posture establishes the offense or defense condition for the
     engagement.

Firer–Table C

     E-21. The firer column identifies which crewmember is to engage the target listed.
     Targets defeated by a different crewmember listed as the firer result in an auto-zero
     penalty, failing to adhere to course conditions.

Weapon (or Ammunition)–Table C

     E-22. The weapon column identifies the weapon (coaxial, TOW, or primary armament
     ammunition type) that is used to defeat the threat. Design of each engagement is for a
     specific weapon or ammunition type (primary armament) for use, which relates to the
     crew conduct of fire, optic selection, engagement techniques, or other unique implied
     tasks. Crews that fail to announce the appropriate weapon or ammunition, or appropriate
     selection for subsequent or supplemental engagements may be penalized IAW
     TC 3-20.31.

366  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                             Table C–Complex Engagements

Target Type–Table C
     E-23. The target type column identifies the target required for the presentation,
     IAW TC 25-8.

Target Posture–Table C
     E-24. The target posture establishes if the target is stationary, moving, or evasive for the
     engagement. See TC 3-20.31 for more information on target postures.

Range (to Target)–Table C
     E-25. The range column identifies the required range (full scale or equivalent scaled
     range) from the firing vehicle to the target. For offensive engagements, this distance
     calculation is from the center of the movement box to the target. The range to target
     distance is provided in meters and is displayed as a whole number, such as 600.
     E-26. The target may be placed at the distance indicated, plus or minus 200 meters. For
     example, when the range to target is listed as 600 meters, the target may be placed
     between 400 and 800 meters. This allows flexibility designing the scenario on varying
     maneuver range facilities with different capabilities, target counts, emplacement
     locations, and terrain restrictions.

Engagement Modifier–Table C
     E-27. No change to the EM is authorized. The range bands listed with their applied
     tolerances do not require any adjustment to the EM. Units are not authorized to exceed
     the target placement tolerances.
     E-28. The EM is the time in seconds required to achieve 100 points against the listed
     threat. To defeat the threat successfully, the crew must achieve the kill standard against
     the target and have an overall engagement time of no more than the EM plus 20 seconds.
     See TC 3-20.31 for the evaluation, timing, and scoring procedures.

                           No Deviations Authorized

          Units are not authorized to deviate from the specified target type,
          posture, range band, firing vehicle posture, or ammunition type.
          This maintains the standards for the complex engagements across
          the force.

Engagements–Table C

     E-29. No deviation is authorized for any condition of the listed tasks. (See figure E-5 on
     page 368 for the conditions for each engagement.)

                     Training Munitions not Resourced

          When units do not have sufficient munitions remaining in their
          ammunition account, they must coordinate with their installation

TRAINING AIDS, DEVICES, SIMULATORS, AND SIMULATIONS –TABLE C

     E-32. This course requires the use of the CMU (some installations may refer to the CMU
     as an IPU for all crews). The CMU is available on the digital range facility scheduled
     (if equipped). Coordination with the scheduled range facility manager will determine
     unit responsibilities for installation, troubleshooting, maintenance, and use of the
     devices ahead of the training day(s). If the range does not have a CMU available, unit
     may draw the CMUR from the TADSS warehouse. The CMUR differs from the CMU
     in that it stores all run data on a solid-state drive on the vehicle, for use with the provided
     AAR suite in the CMUR kit.

     E-33. The brigade commander must approve authorization for deviation from this
     requirement if TADSS are unavailable. Units should focus on training and reinforcing
     the skills in the direct fire kill chain on this table specifically with their thermal optics,
     day and night to best prepare them for the follow-on training events. (See figure E-8 on
     page 372 for the TADSS required or recommended for Table C.)

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  371

Appendix E

     Figure E-8. Table C training aids, devices, simulators, and simulations
                                              requirements

PREPARE FOR TABLE C

     E-34. Units must coordinate for the appropriate training facility, equipment, targetry,
     training munitions, TADSS, and pyrotechnics to support Table C. During the
     preparation phase, the unit should ensure the proofing team, VCEs, and other personnel
     receive training to standard to support their role as evaluators and trainers. This includes
     range officers in charge, range safety officers, and ammunition handlers.

TRAIN THE TRAINERS–TABLE C

     E-35. Typically, units have certified each VC and gunner as VCEs prior entering the
     live-fire density. Units must have certified evaluators to ensure the training objectives
     for the training table are set and to provide effective AARs to each crew.

     E-36. Additional information on leader certification and train the trainer actions is found
     in TC 3-20.31. Units should review and plan for the appropriate training and
     certifications well in advance of the training event.

372         TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                    Table C–Complex Engagements

VEHICLE CREW EVALUATORS–TABLE C

E-37. A trained and certified VCE team must be available to support prior to live fire to
evaluate a gunnery density correctly. After certification, the unit must ensure sufficient
VCEs are available to support the live-fire event. Evaluations of crew gunnery always
come from outside the firing platoon element, and for qualification purposes, VCEs
external to the battalion are required.

E-38. Having the correct number and type of evaluators per firing range maximizes the
unit’s throughput of firing vehicles on the range, reduce range down time, and provide
for the highest quality evaluations. See TC 3-20.31 for more in-depth information on
VCEs, the evaluation team, and the evaluation process.

E-39.   To prepare the trainers, units provide—
     •     Commander training objectives.
     •     Conduct of the range briefing for the training event.
     •     Scenario and lane concept with crew tasks for training.
     •     Risk assessments.
     •     Identification of supporting doctrinal publications for use as primary
           references.
     •     TADSS, pyrotechnics, blanks, and BESs used during the training event (see
           appendix A for the training munitions requirements for the complete training
           strategy).

RECONNAISSANCE OF THE SITE–TABLE C

E-40. Commanders use an authorized training facility for Table C. Commanders follow
the installation’s policies and procedures for formally scheduling the training location
as far in advance as practical. See TC 3-20.31 for more in-depth information concerning
range operations at the unit level.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25         373


---

Appendix E

     E-41. Once scheduled, the commander, Master Gunner, platoon leaders, and platoon
     sergeants conduct a reconnaissance of the training areas to—

          • Verify sufficient space is available to support the entire unit through multiple
                training lanes.

          • Ensure multiple iterations or rotations through the scenario are achievable with
                appropriate retraining opportunities.

          • Ensure sufficient administrative areas are available to support group
                discussions, briefings, and AARs.

          • Provide sand table or terrain board.
          • Identify the ASP.
          • Determine the conduct of the range or the flow of training to maximize the

                live-fire range time.
          • Identify the range operating system and verify the training scenarios have been

                properly loaded.
          • Schedule proofing of the range, based on the higher headquarters OPORD.
          • Ensure requested TADSS are available if provided by the range facility.

ISSUE THE ORDER–TABLE C

     E-42. Commanders can issue an OPORD, WARNORD, or use the training schedule as
     necessary. The WARNORD should include the appropriate tasks, required leaders, and
     equipment needed, as well as the unit’s training timeline including walk-through,
     rehearsals, and AARs. WARNORDs for external organizations must include sufficient
     reaction time for the appropriate personnel and equipment needed.

REHEARSE–TABLE C

     E-43. The unit uses all training resources, materials, and equipment available to conduct
     rehearsals of training events. During the rehearsal, commanders ensure their unit has
     sufficient time to train to the specified standard including AARs and retraining time.

     E-44. Units should rehearse the training event from start to finish, and confirm the
     following:

          • The VCEs understand the training scenarios and tasks.
          • Sufficient space is available to provide appropriate distances for engagements

                ranges and target dispersion.
          • Analog and digital systems are operational within the organization and to the

                higher headquarters regardless of their location.
          • Additional crew training packages (DA Form 8265, DA Form 8265-1, and

                appropriate computer correction factors [zero] documentation) are complete
                and provide for multiple iterations.
          • Sufficient administrative areas are available.
          • Review AAR capabilities and encourage the use of thru-sight video systems to
                increase the fidelity and quality of the AARs.
          • Location of retraining for crews as necessary.

     E-45. Once the unit completes the walk-through and training rehearsal, the commander
     makes any necessary adjustments to the plan and issues FRAGORDs as necessary.

374  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                       Table C–Complex Engagements

                          Proofing the Scenario

     The proofing of the scenario is a demanding process that requires
     a minimum of one training day. It should include all targetry, BESs,
     and sequencing of the approved scenario. Proofing must be
     completed from the firing vehicle type using the optics described in
     the conditions of the firing task.

E-46. The Master Gunner is required to proof the scenario from a Bradley using the
appropriate optics from the established firing positions and firing boxes from the
approved scenario. This is a time-consuming task that will require 1 day to complete to
standard. All targets must be 90-percent visible through the optic directed by the
conditions of each task. For offensive or retrograde engagements, the targets must be
90-percent visible through 90-percent of the maneuver box, at a minimum.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  375


---

Appendix E

EXECUTE–TABLE C, COMPLEX ENGAGEMENTS

     E-47. This section provides a basic overview of the engagements of Table C during
     execution. Units should see TC 3-20.31 for comprehensive information on establishing
     the range, opening instructions, hot and cold times, and other actions necessary to
     conduct live-fire training.

     E-48. Once the unit arrives at the training location, they should—
          • Conduct a safety briefing for the training event.
          • Provide a conduct of the range briefing to establish the sequence of events for
                the training day.
          • Conduct sensitive item checks prior to executing training.
          • Receive briefing from the VCE team.
          • Establish the firing order.
          • Identify the maintenance support area for any fire control or weapons issues on
                the range.
          • Install, align, and place TADSS into operation, as required.

     E-49. The training unit conducts informal AARs of each crew completing a phase of
     training within the training mission. During training, the commander, executive officer,
     1SG, and Master Gunner review the crew’s performance. They follow the unit SOP,
     prescribed tactics, techniques, procedures, and the appropriate T&EOs, and review
     completed crew score sheets and roll-up documentation for clarity and completeness.

             Note. The sequence of the engagements, the number of engagements during
             the day and night phases, and which engagements are fired in a chemical
             environment are determined by the evaluation commander.

                                      Appendix F

             Table M–Missile Qualification

    Table M, Missile Qualification, is a live environment, hands on training
    event using training aids and devices, and includes the use of pyrotechnics
    and BESs. Missile qualification is conducted from a defensive firing
    position. Table M is designed to be conducted with Table III, STX to
    maximize the unit’s time and resources. Table M is conducted in a live
    environment in a training area with established training lanes. This training
    event provides training and evaluation to the crew tactically employing their
    platform as a crew under varying conditions. Table M, Missile
    Qualification, is not a required table, but commanders should conduct
    missile qualification if time and resources allow to maximize the proficiency
    of their BFV crews.

OVERVIEW OF TABLE M

     F-1. Table M verifies the crew’s ability to complete basic crew engagements using
     various platform capabilities, provides for day and night driver’s training as part of the
     unit driver’s training program, and ensures the crew demonstrates proficiency
     performing specific missile engagement procedures on their platform. It provides time
     for training and evaluation of these skills that are the foundation to all maneuver training
     focused on the ability to move, shoot, and communicate.

     F-2. Units should complete Table M for record between T-6 and T-execution (T-week)
     of the live-fire event. Units can include additional tasks they require to support the
     training program but cannot eliminate tasks for any weapon system. While not a
     mandatory part of crew qualification, units should execute Table M during their gunnery
     density whenever resources will allow. Missile proficiency is a critical aspect of a lethal
     BFV crew, and commanders should take every opportunity to improve their crews’
     platform proficiency.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  403


---

Appendix F

     F-3. Table M resides within the BFV training strategy through crew qualification
     (see figure F-1). This is the missile qualification event which demonstrates the crew’s
     ability to successfully employ their platform integrated missile system and effectively
     engage and destroy armored threats on the battlefield. It includes a series of safety
     related tasks related to vehicle safety under various conditions.

                          Figure F-1. Table M, Missile Qualification

                        Conduct Table M with Table III

          Table M is designed to be completed with Table III, STX, to
          maximize time and resources for the unit.

PURPOSE

     F-4. The missile qualification event ensures the crew demonstrates critical crew task
     proficiency in a live environment. The crew executes basic fire and maneuver, integrates
     indirect and direct fires, conducts critical tasks, and completes various emergency
     vehicle tasks prior to conducting the more advanced tasks under live-fire conditions.

404  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

TABLE M METHOD

     F-5. Crew STXs use training areas with multiple training lanes or phases that train
     crew collective movement and engagement techniques at a real-time, full tempo to build
     proficiency. Crew STXs use structured scenarios to replicate the basic engagement
     techniques using the capabilities of their platform. Crews also demonstrate proficiency
     at emergency procedures on their vehicle to build proficiency and confidence in their
     ability to react rapidly to various life-threatening situations while maneuvering their
     platform.

TABLE M END STATE

     F-6. Crews demonstrate in a live environment their ability to execute basic and
     degraded engagement tasks and crew drills in support of decisive action operations. The
     commander sufficiently validated the unit’s vehicle load plan and portions of the SOPs
     for casualty evacuation. Crews received sufficient day and night administrative and
     tactical movement that supports the unit driver’s training program and use of primary
     and alternate battle positions, reverse slope defense positions, and other skills.

     F-7. The commander is confident in the abilities of the subordinate leaders and
     elements to execute those trained tasks to standard on demand based on the tactical
     situation. Live-fire crew training to build experience, proficient, and lethal crews is
     complete.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  405

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

PLAN FOR TABLE M

     F-9. Table M is a live environment, hands on training event using training aids,
     devices, and simulators, and includes the use of blank ammunition, pyrotechnics, and
     BESs. STX is a station-based event that consists of a firing platoon, critical tasks
     platoon, and training support platoon.

     F-10. This section details the resources needed for planning purposes. It includes
     general information on the following topics for consideration:

          • Uniform—the minimum uniform requirements for the training event.
          • Facilities—available facilities that support the training event.
          • Training plan—the minimum tasks, general guidelines, and special instructions

                for the training event.
          • Time and throughput—the estimated time required to complete the training

                event including throughput considerations for planning.
          • Ammunition—any training munitions required to complete the training event

                (this includes DDI items that require coordination).
          • TADSS—any available training aids that directly support or augment the

                training event.

     F-11. Table M is the recommended missile qualification within the crew platform
     strategy. Units should complete Table M between T-6 and T-week to support the training
     events.

     F-12. Units may require coordination with installation support activities such as the TSC
     for devices or training aids, or the ASP for DDI items. Each installation’s policies and
     procedures vary. Units should reference their supporting installation’s training SOPs or
     policies for detailed information.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  407

TABLE M TRAINING PLAN

     F-15. Units should maximize the use of the training resources available to conduct as

     Table M is designed to integrate into the unit’s Table III, STX event. (See figure F-5 for
     an example layout for a round-robin event by platoon.)

     Figure F-5. Table M round-robin example

410  TC 3-20.31-25                            23 July 2025

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

Table M Firing Group

     F-16. Performance of the direct fire engagements portion of the STX (firing group) is
     on the crew’s vehicle. Table design is set up to train and evaluate the crew’s ability to
     engage stationary or evasive moving targets placed in a tactical array, during day and
     limited visibility conditions. Crews execute these tasks from the offense and defense.

     F-17. The engagements may be full-scale or scaled, depending on the usable area within
     the training area. Crew’s target acquisition skills and engagement techniques, using
     3/4-scale targets, is challenged. See TC 25-8 for additional information on target
     dimensions for scaled ranges.

     F-18. All engagements are evaluated using certified VCE’s using DA Form 8265 and
     DA Form 8265-1 as the official record of training. Master Gunners must ensure the
     training records are included in each crew’s gunnery packet.

     F-19. The engagements listings are by capability type using the standard engagement
     naming structure listed in TC 3-20.31. It provides the requirements for each engagement,
     which are described in paragraphs F-20 through F-28.

Task Description–Table M

     F-20. The task description includes both the engagement number (the table number plus
     engagement task) followed by the system capability trained.

Firing Vehicle Posture–Table M

     F-21. The firing vehicle posture establishes the offense or defense condition for the
     engagement.

Firer–Table M

     F-22. The firer column identifies which crewmember is to engage the target listed.
     Targets defeated by a different crewmember listed as the firer result in an auto-zero
     penalty, failing to adhere to course conditions.

Weapon (or Ammunition)–Table M

     F-23. The weapon column identifies the weapon (coaxial or primary armament
     ammunition type) that is used to defeat the threat. Design of each engagement is for a
     specific weapon or ammunition type (primary armament) for use, which relates to the
     crew conduct of fire, optic selection, engagement techniques, or other unique implied
     tasks. Crews that fail to announce the appropriate weapon or ammunition, or appropriate
     selection for subsequent or supplemental engagements may be penalized
     IAW TC 3-20.31.

Target Type–Table M

     F-24. The target type is the target required for the presentation, IAW TC 25-8.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  411


---

Appendix F

Target Posture–Table M

     F-25. The target posture establishes if the target is stationary, moving, or evasive for the
     engagement. See TC 3-20.31 for more information on target postures.

Range (to Target)–Table M

     F-26. The required range (full scale or equivalent scaled range) from the firing vehicle
     to the target. For offensive engagements, this distance calculation is from the center of
     the movement box to the target. The range to target distance is provided in meters, and
     may be displayed as a whole number distance, (for example, 600). The target may be
     placed at the distance indicated, plus or minus 200 meters. For example, the target may
     be placed between 400 and 800 meters. No change to the EM is authorized.

Engagement Modifier–Table M

     F-27. The EM is the time in seconds required to achieve 100 points against the listed
     threat. To defeat the threat successfully, the crew must achieve the kill standard against
     the target and have an overall engagement time of no more than the EM plus 20 seconds.
     See TC 3-20.31 for the evaluation, timing, and scoring procedures.

Missile Time of Flight

     F-28. The missile time of flight section identifies the time of flight for the selected
     missile based on range to target. This time starts when the ATWESS charge is fired
     (trigger squeeze plus 1 second built-in test) and ends when a live missile would arrive
     at the target location. This time is used by the target operator to trigger effects on target
     through means of a BES and lower the target presentation that has been properly
     engaged by the crew. During this time the firer of the missile must execute and maintain
     proper target tracking techniques as assessed by the VCE.

412  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

Scenario Requirements

     F-29. Units may not deviate from any target type, target posture, range, and weapon
     system/ammunition type for Table M. Engagements firing may be in any sequence that
     best supports their training area, flow-of the range, and time available.

     F-30. The unit options and requirements are—
          • Determining the number of engagements conducted during the day phase and
                night phase.
          • Determining the sequence of the engagements.
          • Using BESs as described in TC 3-20.31 for all target panel and troop set
                presentations.
          • Using all target panels at 3/4 scale at full range distances.
          • Using available TADSS, including laser-based systems, and LTIDs (additional
                use of TADSS is at the discretion of the unit commander).

     F-31. Additional information concerning the engagements, the terms of reference, or
     the layout for each event’s scenario can be found in TC 3-20.31. (See figure F-6 on
     page 414 for the engagements for the firing group.)

     F-32. This table is designed to be fired from the baseline prior to the crew’s day/night
     run of Table III, STX. The crew executes their day or night TOW engagements prior to
     executing their day or night Table III iteration.

     F-33. The requirements for the missile qualification are—
          • Selecting one CBRN engagement day or night.
          • Executing the engagement as a stand-alone course of fire, if range and time will
                allow.
          • Performing crew execution of TOW reload procedures from their battle
                position IAW GST standards.
          • Firing one offensive TOW-on-the-move engagement day and night if BFVs
                have TOW-on-the-move capability.

TIME AND THROUGHPUT–TABLE M

     F-37. The throughput expectation of this table is 14 crews per day for two consecutive
     days. This allows for the appropriate amount of training, retraining, and cross training
     to provide the commander the fullest flexibility for crew management options and talent
     management opportunities prior to live fire. Depending on maintenance or other
     unforeseen issues, recommend incorporating an additional half training day to the
     schedule, although this not resourced as part of the Combined Arms Training Strategy.
     (See figure F-8 for the expected throughput schedule based on the number of vehicles
     for firing and the unit’s day/night engagement breakdown for the engagements.)

     4 hours of target maintenance down time (two blocks of 2 hours each). These
     calculations are for planning purposes only. Units that are more proficient in
     tower operations may have an increased throughput. The critical task and
     call-for requirements (not shown) are conducted simultaneously with the
     more time-consuming engagement skills tasks.

TRAINING AIDS, DEVICES, SIMULATORS, SIMULATIONS–TABLE M

     F-39. This course requires the use of laser based TADSS for all crews. Units with

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

Instrumentable-Multiple Integrated Laser Engagement System CVTESS–
Table M

     F-40. The I-MILES CVTESS is a laser-based training device to for use on Abrams,
     Bradley, medium tank, and opposing forces tanks and fighting vehicles to provide real
     time casualty effects. I-MILES CVTESS is an evolutionary approach for replacing older
     systems with equipment that provides better training fidelity. It will reinforce crew
     duties, reward proper engagement techniques, and develop tactical maneuver skills of
     armor and mechanized infantry combined arms teams up to brigade level. It provides
     unit commanders an integrated training system in force-on-force and force-on-target
     training events at home station training areas through instrumented training. The system
     interfaces with instrumentation systems at combat training centers.

Laser Target Interface Device–Table M

     F-41. For use with I-MILES CVTESS or other laser-based training systems when the
     unit is conducting force-on-target training, the LTIDs provides the appropriate hit
     recording at the target emplacement and when stimulated, signals the target lifting
     mechanism to record a hit. This is a required device on Table III.

Crew Module Unit/Crew Module Unit Recorder–Table M

     F-42. The CMU/IPU/CMUR is an advanced eight channel audio/video recording device
     for combat vehicles. The CMU/IPU/CMUR can simultaneously record up to eight
     channels of video, one channel of audio, and has the capability to monitor dual 1553
     data busses. The CMU/IPU/CMUR is equipped with a Global Positioning System that
     captures the vehicle’s position location information. The CMU/IPU is for use on a digital
     range complex whereas the CMUR does not require any external support. The CMUR
     has an integrated AAR suite and stores information on a solid-state drive. The AAR
     suite uploads the data from the solid-state drive allowing a full AAR presentation.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  419

Appendix F

PREPARE TABLE M

     F-43. Units execute the crew STX in a variety of ways. Typically, each platoon conducts
     training independently prior to the crew STX event, either in the motor pool, simulator
     system, or in a local training area. The primary trainers during the crew STX are the
     company commander with OC/Ts providing oversight and assistance as necessary.

TRAIN THE TRAINERS–TABLE M

     F-44. Units execute the crew STX in a variety of ways. Typically, each platoon conducts
     training independently prior to the crew STX event, either in the motor pool, simulator
     system, or in a local training area. The primary trainers during the crew STX are the
     company commander with OC/Ts providing oversight and assistance as necessary.

             Note. Much like crew platform evaluators, the OC/Ts for any crew exercise
             provide exceptional training for the OC/T as a leader, second only to
             executing the task with their organization. Units should dedicate sufficient
             time to OC/T certification to leaders who can provide experienced
             mentorship, with the complete understanding that their OC/T task is also
             exceptional training for them.

     F-45.   Units provide the following to prepare the trainers:
          •     Commander’s training objectives.
          •     Scenario and lane concept with crew tasks.
          •     Conduct of the range briefing for the training event.
          •     Review of T&EOs and evaluation process for all the tasks.
          •     Risk assessment.
          •     Identification of supporting doctrinal publications for use as primary
                references.
          •     TADSS, pyrotechnics, blanks, and BESs used during the training event
                (see appendix A for the training munitions requirements for the complete
                training strategy).

ENGAGEMENT TASK AND PURPOSE–TABLE M

     F-46. The crews, leaders, and evaluators must understand the task and purpose of the
     engagements, the critical skills tasks, and how they are linked to their training. Each of
     the engagements should be reviewed to understand what they contain, the standards for
     each, and how they build from previous training and to the next event’s engagements.

420          TC 3-20.31-25           23 July 2025


---

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

RECONNAISSANCE OF THE SITE–TABLE M

     F-47. Commanders use an authorized training area or series of training areas for the
     Table M, STX, Missile Qualification. Commanders follow the installation’s policies and
     procedures for scheduling the training location as far in advance as practical. Once
     commanders have established the method of training and evaluation, they should secure
     the training location and appropriate TADSS, equipment, or systems that enhance the
     training event.

     F-48. Once scheduled, the commander, Master Gunner, platoon leaders, and platoon
     sergeants conduct a reconnaissance of the training areas to—

          • Verify sufficient space is available to support the entire unit through multiple
                training lanes.

          • Ensure multiple iterations or rotations through the scenario can be achieved
                with appropriate retraining opportunities.

          • Ensure sufficient administrative areas are available to support group
                discussions, briefings, and AARs.

          • Provide sand table or terrain board.
          • Provide terrain board kits.
          • Verify the training area can support the unit’s training while in a chemical

                environment, based on the scenario design (protection).
          • Verify casualty evacuation and medical evacuation notional capabilities in

                support of the training scenario, for which units mostly include only—
                 FM reporting.
                 Request procedures for training purposes (sustainment).

ISSUE THE ORDER–TABLE M

     F-49. Commanders can issue an OPORD, WARNORD, or use the training schedule as
     necessary. The WARNORD should include the appropriate tasks, required leaders, and
     equipment needed, as well as the unit’s training timeline including walk-through,
     rehearsals, and AARs. WARNORDs for external organizations must include sufficient
     reaction time for the appropriate personnel and equipment needed.

     F-50. The OPORD or WARNORD should include the list of tasks that commanders
     evaluate during the event, and the method of evaluation. Commanders should provide
     sufficient time for subordinate leaders to train the tasks and skills listed in the order.
     Commanders should ensure the appropriate training resources are available and
     accessible. Upon receipt, subordinate leaders that have SOPs for their organizations
     must ensure their procedures support and complement the higher headquarters
     publication.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  421

Appendix F

REHEARSE–TABLE M

     F-51. The unit uses all training resources, materials, and equipment available to conduct
     rehearsals of training events. During the rehearsal, commanders ensure their unit has
     sufficient time to train to the specified standard including AARs and retraining time.
     Commanders ensure their unit verifies optional scenarios that increase complexity when
     warranted.

     F-52. Commander uses the rehearsal as an opportunity to verify the tasks selected for
     Table M are consistent with the commander’s training guidance.

     F-53.   Units should rehearse the training event from start to finish, and confirm—
          •     Sufficient space is available to provide appropriate distances for offensive
                engagements.
          •     Analog and digital systems are operational within the organization and to the
                higher headquarters regardless of their location.
          •     Sufficient administrative areas are available.
          •     AAR capabilities are reviewed.
          •     The unit is aware of methods and actions required to provide fire support
                (notional).
          •     Integration of attack aviation and close air support during the scenario (notional
                inclusion for call-for tasks).
          •     Location of retraining for crews as necessary.
          •     VCE certification on tasks associated with missile engagements.

     F-54. Once the unit completes the walk-through and training rehearsal, the commander
     makes any necessary adjustments to the plan and issues FRAGORDs as necessary.

422               TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025

EXECUTE –TABLE M

     F-55. Once the unit arrives at the training location, they should—
          • Conduct a safety briefing for the training event.
          • Provide a conduct of the range briefing to establish the sequence of events for
                the training day.
          • Conduct sensitive item checks prior to executing training.
          • Assign VCEs as appropriate.
          • Issue the OPORD for the training mission.
          • Execute the training scenario.

     F-56. The training unit conducts informal AARs of each crew completing a phase of
     training within the training mission. During training, the assigned VCE, executive
     officer, 1SG, and Master Gunner review the subordinate unit’s performance. They
     follow the unit SOP, prescribed TTP, and the appropriate T&EOs, and review completed
     crew score sheets and roll-up documentation for clarity and completeness.

     F-57. The unit’s scenario sequence will vary based on the training area available, time,
     day/night mix of engagements, and determination of which day and night engagements
     include operating in a chemical environment. (See figure F-11 on page 424 through F-30
     on page 443 for the task, conditions, and standards for each engagement sequentially.)

Appendix F

Engagement Modifier–Table M

     F-74. The EM for a missile engagement is calculated using Bradley as the firer in
     DA Form 8265 page 2, block 1. Blocks 2-4 are used in the calculation of the EM.
     Block 5 on DA Form 8265 page 2, range to target is not used when calculating EMs for
     missile engagements. The EMs for Table M, Missile Qualification are included in the
     scenario requirements and no deviations are authorized.

Defilade

     F-75. Missiles can be fired from the defilade, and no defilade time is authorized,
     regardless of if the crew chooses to move to the enfilade position prior to firing or not.

Break

     F-76. Break or target break time on missile engagements applies only to multiple target
     presentations. No target break time is authorized on single target presentations or for the
     first target engaged in multiple target engagements. The break time for second, third,
     and fourth target presentations is the calculated using the time elapsed at the moment
     the crew has destroyed the previous target engaged.

448  TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                           Table M–Missile Qualification

F-77. The break time for Target 2, is recorded as the time the crew destroyed Target 1
(see figure F-34). The timing controller marks this time when the target operator
indicates a successful target destruction to the crew by firing the BES and lowering the
target engaged. Assuming the crew has successfully engaged and destroyed the target,
this should be the kill time from block 13a plus the time of flight for the missile listed
in the scenario requirements.

Appendix F

Engagement Time

     F-78. Engagement time is calculated by following the instructions on the
     DA Form 8265 and subtracting the modifier, defilade, and break times from the kill
     time. The values are then aligned with the corresponding point values below the target
     engagement time shown (see figure F-35).

                             Table M–Missile Qualification

Crew Evaluation During Missile Time of Flight

     F-79. The most critical events of a missile engagement take place after the trigger
     squeeze. During the missile time of flight, the VCE is evaluating the missile firer on
     their ability to maintain their reticle on the target. If at any time the reticle is removed
     from the target, the firer will have failed to destroy the target and will have to attempt to
     reengage.

     F-80. If during the missile time of flight, the firer, or VC perform a wire cut and aborts
     the missile flight, the crew will have failed to destroy the target and will have to attempt
     to reengage.

     F-81. If the crew fails to track the target or performs a wire cut, the VCE will announce
     to the target controller that this has occurred, and the operator will not trigger the effects
     simulator or lower the target presentation. No reengagement of a target is authorized,
     and the crew will receive zero points for any missed targets.

     F-82. If target exposure time ends prior to the completion of the prescribed missile time
     of flight for the engaged target, the crew will have failed to destroy the target and will
     receive zero points on that target presentation.

Missile Reloads

     F-83. Reloading the TOW missile is a critical task associated with missile engagements
     and proficiency. Crews must demonstrate their ability to quickly and safely reload their
     own missiles and continue to engage the threats presented. The VCE will evaluate this
     task and record points of success or failure on the DA Form 8265 and review them with
     the crew during the AAR. Any safety violation will result in the crew receiving an
     auto-zero for that engagement.

FIRING GROUP AFTER ACTION REVIEWS–TABLE M

     F-84. For the firing group, each crew is given an AAR at the completion of the day
     phase and night phase by a qualified VCE. Use DA Form 8265-1 to record and track the
     results of each crew’s performance on each engagement at the completion of each phase.
     Tracking crew and platoon statistics on each engagement allow the unit leadership to
     identify areas for improvement.

             Note. VCEs must be certified or recertified as described in TC 3-20.31.
             Performing the duties as a VCE during the conduct of Table III, STX is a
             significant part of building experience and knowledge of those gunners and
             VCs without firing.

     F-85. Units should track the various firing information from the score sheets to identify
     where in the conduct of fire crews and the overall unit should apply additional training
     effort. Some of those indicators are—

          • Target acquisition, detection, and identification.
          • Time to fire.
          • Time to kill.
          • Opening and closing times for machine gun engagements.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  451


---

Appendix F

          • Gunner lay errors.
          • Scanning techniques.
          • Engagement techniques.
          • Conduct of fire issues.
          • Penalties by type.

     F-86. Each crew should receive a formal AAR following the guidelines found in
     TC 3-20.31. Each evaluator should use DA Form 8265 and DA Form 8265-1 as the
     basis for their evaluation, identifying the firing occasional information from the score
     sheet timelines. The evaluator and VC each review and sign the DA Form 8265-1.

CRITICAL SKILLS TASK AFTER ACTION REVIEWS–TABLE M

     F-87. OC/Ts should provide clear and articulate comments and insight about the
     positive and negative actions of the crews they observed. OC/Ts should use the T&EOs
     to guide them through the evaluation process and use the doctrinal publications to
     support their comments.

     F-88. The OC/T and unit leadership should review the T&EOs and training notes and
     observations to determine which tasks require additional attention through the remainder
     of the training density.

     F-89. The OC/T should share the overall performance of the unit, including the
     observations, with the entire training unit. This allows the subordinate units to learn from
     other unit’s best practices, procedures, and tactics, and at times from their shortcomings
     or mistakes. The commander should identify crews within the organization that
     demonstrate superior performance.

     F-90. At the completion of this portion of crew Table M, Missile Qualification,
     commanders, executive officers, and 1SGs should gain insight to where their unit’s
     SOPs are working and where they need additional attention. If time is available,
     commanders should update and provide those changes or revisions to the unit prior to
     executing any live-fire event. Of particular interest is the drivers’ training program,
     vehicle load plans, stowage of individual tactical gear, crew evacuation procedures, and
     medical evacuation procedures.

RETRAIN–TABLE M

     F-91. The unit Master Gunner maintains the unit roll-up by engagement. A
     subcategorized roll-up by platoons provides the commander a snapshot of training
     trends, strengths, and weaknesses. The Master Gunner should not only identify crews
     that did not achieve a passing standard, but also crews that struggled on specific
     engagement tasks. Once done, the unit can establish and conduct the appropriate focused
     retraining followed by a subsequent evaluation.

     F-92. All crews must successfully complete the course of fire based on the standards for
     the engagements listed earlier in this chapter. No crew may proceed to Table IV or any
     other live-fire event, including zeroing, or sub-caliber events until they have achieved
     the minimum standard on Table III.

     F-93. Platoon sergeants and platoon leaders should identify crews that require additional
     training on the various crew critical skills. Although the evaluation may be completed,

452         TC 3-20.31-25  23 July 2025


---

                                                                                 Table M–Missile Qualification

     units should integrate sustainment training into their unit training plans, particularly
     when environmental conditions change, movement during limited visibility is required,
     or planning or expecting tactical movement through restricted or rugged terrain.

DRIVER’S TRAINING–TABLE M

     F-94. Leaders should determine the number of miles driven during the day and night
     phases for their assigned drivers. This information should be recorded, including the
     miles driven, miles driven with the enhanced viewer, and any tactical (off-road) miles
     driven.

CREW GUNNERY FOLDER RECORDS–TABLE M

     F-95. Once testing is complete, the Master Gunner records the results of all training
     completed in the crew gunnery folder. The records must be maintained for a minimum
     of 12 months.

RECORD INDIVIDUAL TASK PERFORMANCE–TABLE M

     F-96. Leaders track and train their Soldier’s individual and collective tasks trained and
     evaluated using tools provided by the ATN such as the SULT and the DJB. These
     systems interface directly with the DTMS to record training and evaluation results.

             Note. Leaders must be granted access first by their commander or DTMS
             manager to enroll or assign their Soldiers the specific tasks from the ICTL
             that are trained and tested during the training event.

     F-97. From within the SULT, the authorized leader is enabled to view, maintain, and
     update—

          • Warrior tasks and battle drills.
          • MOS-specific individual critical tasks.
          • Unit-mandated training tasks.
          • Supporting T&EOs (access dependent on proponent distribution restrictions).

     F-98. Leaders should update their Soldier’s individual training records for training and
     evaluation of the following tasks, based on their assigned position. This is not an
     all-inclusive list of the trained and tested tasks for this event. Leaders should adjust
     based on the unit’s additions to the event.

     F-99. This ensures historical data is available throughout the Army force generation
     training cycle for the chain of command to assess strengths and weaknesses of crews
     and address these concerns in upcoming GST training. The most current GST records
     must accompany the unit or crew throughout the entire weapons training density.

UPDATING INDIVIDUAL TRAINING RECORDS–TABLE M

     F-100. Leaders update their SULT application with the appropriate training and
     evaluation outcomes for their crewmembers. The lists provided show the touch points
     associated with completing Table M.

23 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-25  453

Appendix F

     F-101. Leaders must determine if the tasks were effectively trained to the Soldier and
     the level of proficiency based upon their performance. Leaders then update the tasks for
     each Soldier based on their assessment. (See tasks that are included within this event for
     the crew in figure F-36 through page 458.)

AUX             auxiliary

BES             battle effects simulator

CBRN            chemical, biological, radiological, or nuclear

CMUR            crew module unit recorder

DDI             dummy, drilled, inert

DJB             digital job book

DTMS            Digital Training Management System

DVO             direct view optic

EM              engagement modifier

FM              field manual, frequency modulation

FRAGORD         fragmentary order

GST             gunnery skills test

GTLF            gate to live fire

HITS            home station/Army mobile instrumentation training system

I-MILES CVTESS  instrumentable–multiple integrated laser engagement system combat vehicle
                tactical engagement simulation system

IAW             in accordance with

IPU             integrated player unit
